I just wake up on my own, or from some small noise in the house, like I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tired or fatigued during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. By the time I finish seeing my home-health clients and come back to help out at my own house, my body just feels... heavy. Even doing simple things like charting my notes or folding a load of laundry can feel like it's just too much to push through.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge during the day?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious. I don't really get that on-edge feeling. Like I was saying earlier, I just feel really drained most of the time. I don't really have the energy to feel nervous or keyed up, honestly... I'm just focused on getting through my routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you work non-traditional hours or a variable shift schedule?\n\nPatient: No, my hours are pretty set. I work a standard day shift... usually just morning to late afternoon seeing my clients. The schedule doesn't really change from week to week, so I don't do any night shifts or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you go to bed and wake up at the same time every day, including weekends?\n\nPatient: I don't really keep track of it that closely, to be honest. I mean, during the week I have to get up around the same time for work, but my bedtime kind of varies depending on when I finally run out of steam. And on the weekends, I don't really stick to a set schedule at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep when you first go to bed?\n\nPatient: Falling asleep isn't really the issue. Since I'm so drained, I usually just fall into bed completely exhausted. \n\nThe problem is more that my sleep is just... broken, and it has been for a long time now. Once I'm asleep, I wake up to every little sound in the house. And then I almost always end up waking up before my alarm even goes off, just feeling like I never really rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually wake up in the middle of the night and have trouble going back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, that's exactly it. My sleep is just so broken. I'll wake up in the middle of the night to every little sound in the house, and once I'm awake, it's really hard to settle back down. I just kind of lay there, and then I'm up for good before my alarm even goes off, feeling like I never really got any rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you go to bed and wake up at the same time every day, even on weekends?\n\nPatient: I think you might have just asked me that a minute ago. But no, like I was saying, I don't really keep a strict schedule. \n\nDuring the week I have to get up at the same time for work, but I just go to bed whenever I'm too tired to keep my eyes open. And on the weekends, no, I don't wake up or go to sleep at the same time at all. It just varies.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I fall into bed completely exhausted, but then I wake up to every little sound in the house. And then I'm up way before my alarm even goes off, just feeling like I never really rested.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot during the day?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have normal everyday things I think about, but I wouldn't say I'm worrying a lot. Honestly, I'm usually just too tired for my mind to be racing like that. I mostly just focus on getting through my shift and making it through the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you have bad dreams or nightmares that wake you up?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I rarely remember my dreams anyway. So it's not nightmares waking me up... I just wake up on my own, or from some small noise in the house, like I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tired or fatigued during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. By the time I finish seeing my home-health clients and come back to help out at my own house, my body just feels... heavy. Even doing simple things like charting my notes or folding a load of laundry can feel like it's just too much to push through.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge during the day?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious. I don't really get that on-edge feeling. Like I was saying earlier, I just feel really drained most of the time. I don't really have the energy to feel nervous or keyed up, honestly... I'm just focused on getting through my routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you work non-traditional hours or a variable shift schedule?\n\nPatient: No, my hours are pretty set. I work a standard day shift... usually just morning to late afternoon seeing my clients. The schedule doesn't really change from week to week, so I don't do any night shifts or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you go to bed and wake up at the same time every day, including weekends?\n\nPatient: I don't really keep track of it that closely, to be honest. I mean, during the week I have to get up around the same time for work, but my bedtime kind of varies depending on when I finally run out of steam. And on the weekends, I don't really stick to a set schedule at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep when you first go to bed?\n\nPatient: Falling asleep isn't really the issue. Since I'm so drained, I usually just fall into bed completely exhausted. \n\nThe problem is more that my sleep is just... broken, and it has been for a long time now. Once I'm asleep, I wake up to every little sound in the house. And then I almost always end up waking up before my alarm even goes off, just feeling like I never really rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually wake up in the middle of the night and have trouble going back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, that's exactly it. My sleep is just so broken. I'll wake up in the middle of the night to every little sound in the house, and once I'm awake, it's really hard to settle back down. I just kind of lay there, and then I'm up for good before my alarm even goes off, feeling like I never really got any rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you go to bed and wake up at the same time every day, even on weekends?\n\nPatient: I think you might have just asked me that a minute ago. But no, like I was saying, I don't really keep a strict schedule. \n\nDuring the week I have to get up at the same time for work, but I just go to bed whenever I'm too tired to keep my eyes open. And on the weekends, no, I don't wake up or go to sleep at the same time at all. It just varies.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're waking up early because you want to, or because you just can't sleep anymore?\n\nPatient: It's definitely because I just can't sleep anymore. I would love to stay asleep. But like I was saying, my sleep has just been so broken for a long time now. I end up awake before the alarm, and my body just won't let me drift off again... even though I still feel completely exhausted, like I didn't get any real rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep problems started after any significant changes in your life or work?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. This whole thing started when my mother-in-law moved in with us. That was a little over two years ago.\n\nDoctor: Has your sleep improved at any point since your mother-in-law moved in, such as when she was away from your home for an extended period?\n\nPatient: She hasn't really been away since she got here, to be honest. I'm just focused on getting through my routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you work non-traditional hours or a variable shift schedule?\n\nPatient: No, my hours are pretty set. I work a standard day shift... usually just morning to late afternoon seeing my clients. I mean, during the week I have to get up around the same time for work, but my bedtime kind of varies depending on when I finally run out of steam. And on the weekends, I don't really stick to a set schedule at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep when you first go to bed?\n\nPatient: Falling asleep isn't really the issue. Once I'm asleep, I wake up to every little sound in the house. And then I almost always end up waking up before my alarm even goes off, just feeling like I never really rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually wake up in the middle of the night and have trouble going back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, that's exactly it. My sleep is just so broken. I'll wake up in the middle of the night to every little sound in the house, and once I'm awake, it's really hard to settle back down. I just kind of lay there, and then I'm up for good before my alarm even goes off, feeling like I never really got any rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you go to bed and wake up at the same time every day, even on weekends?\n\nPatient: I think you might have just asked me that a minute ago. But no, like I was saying, I don't really keep a strict schedule. \n\nDuring the week I have to get up at the same time for work, but I just go to bed whenever I'm too tired to keep my eyes open. And on the weekends, no, I don't wake up or go to sleep at the same time at all. It just varies.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're waking up early because you want to, or because you just can't sleep anymore?\n\nPatient: It's definitely because I just can't sleep anymore. I would love to stay asleep. But like I was saying, my sleep has just been so broken for a long time now. I end up awake before the alarm, and my body just won't let me drift off again... even though I still feel completely exhausted, like I didn't get any real rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep problems started after any significant changes in your life or work?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. This whole thing started when my mother-in-law moved in with us. That was a little over two years ago.\n\nDoctor: Has your sleep improved at any point since your mother-in-law moved in, such as when she was away from your home for an extended period?\n\nPatient: She hasn't really been away since she got here, to be honest. She needs a lot of help, so she's just... always there. So no, my sleep hasn't really had a chance to improve. It's just been the same this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the stress of having your mother-in-law living with you is contributing to your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: I mean, probably? It's hard to say for sure if it's just the stress of her being there, or... I don't know, maybe it's just everything combined. But it definitely feels like my sleep just went downhill right around the time she moved in, and it hasn't gotten any better since. So, yeah, I guess it has to be connected somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel overwhelmed or stressed when you're lying in bed at night?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I'm not lying there running through a list of worries or anything, if that's what you mean. I don't feel actively stressed out in the moment. It's just... I just lie there and can't seem to drift off. So I wouldn't say I feel overwhelmed right then, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your mother-in-law or the stress of caring for her when you're lying awake at night?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Like I was just saying, I'm not really lying there worrying about things. I don't lay in bed thinking about her or all the extra work she needs. It's more like... I just can't sleep. My mind isn't racing with thoughts about the stress of it all, it's just kind of blank, but awake.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep would improve if you kept the same bedtime and wake-up time every day, including weekends?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. And on the weekends, no, I don't wake up or go to sleep at the same time at all. It just varies.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're waking up early because you want to, or because you just can't sleep anymore?\n\nPatient: It's definitely because I just can't sleep anymore. I would love to stay asleep. But like I was saying, my sleep has just been so broken for a long time now. I end up awake before the alarm, and my body just won't let me drift off again... even though I still feel completely exhausted, like I didn't get any real rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep problems started after any significant changes in your life or work?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. This whole thing started when my mother-in-law moved in with us. That was a little over two years ago.\n\nDoctor: Has your sleep improved at any point since your mother-in-law moved in, such as when she was away from your home for an extended period?\n\nPatient: She hasn't really been away since she got here, to be honest. She needs a lot of help, so she's just... always there. So no, my sleep hasn't really had a chance to improve. It's just been the same this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the stress of having your mother-in-law living with you is contributing to your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: I mean, probably? It's hard to say for sure if it's just the stress of her being there, or... I don't know, maybe it's just everything combined. But it definitely feels like my sleep just went downhill right around the time she moved in, and it hasn't gotten any better since. So, yeah, I guess it has to be connected somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel overwhelmed or stressed when you're lying in bed at night?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I'm not lying there running through a list of worries or anything, if that's what you mean. I don't feel actively stressed out in the moment. It's just... I just lie there and can't seem to drift off. So I wouldn't say I feel overwhelmed right then, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your mother-in-law or the stress of caring for her when you're lying awake at night?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Like I was just saying, I'm not really lying there worrying about things. I don't lay in bed thinking about her or all the extra work she needs. It's more like... I just can't sleep. My mind isn't racing with thoughts about the stress of it all, it's just kind of blank, but awake.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep would improve if you kept the same bedtime and wake-up time every day, including weekends?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't really tried it, to be honest. My schedule kind of shifts around depending on my shifts for work and what's going on here at the house, so I've never really stuck to a strict routine like that. I'm not sure if it would make a difference or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep problems would improve if you were to travel to a different time zone?\n\nPatient: I have no idea, honestly. Um, I haven't traveled anywhere in a really long time, let alone to a different time zone. Between my shifts and taking care of things here at the house, going on a trip just isn't something I do. So... yeah, I really couldn't tell you if that would make a difference or not. I've never had the chance to test it out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body has a natural wake-up time that it wants to follow, regardless of when you go to bed?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't noticed a specific time my body naturally wants to wake up. With my work schedule and everything going on with my mother-in-law, my sleep is just kind of all over the place. I just know that when I do wake up in the middle of the night, I can't get back to sleep, no matter what time it is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's just been the same this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the stress of having your mother-in-law living with you is contributing to your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: I mean, probably? It's hard to say for sure if it's just the stress of her being there, or... I don't know, maybe it's just everything combined. But it definitely feels like my sleep just went downhill right around the time she moved in, and it hasn't gotten any better since. So, yeah, I guess it has to be connected somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel overwhelmed or stressed when you're lying in bed at night?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I'm not lying there running through a list of worries or anything, if that's what you mean. I don't feel actively stressed out in the moment. It's just... I just lie there and can't seem to drift off. So I wouldn't say I feel overwhelmed right then, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your mother-in-law or the stress of caring for her when you're lying awake at night?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Like I was just saying, I'm not really lying there worrying about things. I don't lay in bed thinking about her or all the extra work she needs. It's more like... I just can't sleep. My mind isn't racing with thoughts about the stress of it all, it's just kind of blank, but awake.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep would improve if you kept the same bedtime and wake-up time every day, including weekends?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't really tried it, to be honest. My schedule kind of shifts around depending on my shifts for work and what's going on here at the house, so I've never really stuck to a strict routine like that. I'm not sure if it would make a difference or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep problems would improve if you were to travel to a different time zone?\n\nPatient: I have no idea, honestly. Um, I haven't traveled anywhere in a really long time, let alone to a different time zone. Between my shifts and taking care of things here at the house, going on a trip just isn't something I do. So... yeah, I really couldn't tell you if that would make a difference or not. I've never had the chance to test it out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body has a natural wake-up time that it wants to follow, regardless of when you go to bed?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't noticed a specific time my body naturally wants to wake up. With my work schedule and everything going on with my mother-in-law, my sleep is just kind of all over the place. I just know that when I do wake up in the middle of the night, I can't get back to sleep, no matter what time it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep problems are related to not having a regular sleep schedule?\n\nPatient: It's hard to say. Um... since my schedule is always changing with my shifts and everything else, I don't really have a normal routine to compare it to. It might be part of the reason, but I honestly don't know if that's the main cause of it."
